      241 +// tabbarV2Boxed reproduces the old-project style: boxed activ
          +e tab, hidden                                                 
      242 +// borders on inactive tabs, superscript number hotkeys (press
          + 1-9/0 to jump).                                              
      243 +func tabbarV2Boxed(width, height int) string {                
      244 +  names := []string{"Folders", "This Device", "Remote Devices"
          +, "Alerts(2)", "Actions"}                                     
      245 +  sups := []string{"¹", "²", "³", "⁴", "⁵"}                   
      246 +  inactive := lipgloss.NewStyle().Padding(0, 1).Border(lipglos
          +s.HiddenBorder())                                             
      247 +  activeSt := lipgloss.NewStyle().Padding(0, 1).Border(lipglos
          +s.NormalBorder()).BorderForeground(accent)                    
      248 +  tabs := make([]string, len(names))                          
      249 +  for i, n := range names {                                   
      250 +    style := inactive                                         
      251 +    if i == 0 {                                               
      252 +      style = activeSt                                        
      253 +    }                                                         
      254 +    tabs[i] = style.Render(n + dimStyle.Render(sups[i]))      
      255 +  }                                                           
      256 +  top := lipgloss.JoinHorizontal(lipgloss.Bottom, tabs...)    
      257 +  // reuse the V2 table body below the boxed tab row          
      258 +  inner := foldersV2Table(width, height-lipgloss.Height(top)+2
          +)                                                             
      259 +  body := strings.SplitN(inner, "\n", 3)[2] // strip its own u
          +nderline tab bar                                              
      260 +  return lipgloss.JoinVertical(lipgloss.Left, top, body)      
      261 +}

● Done reviewing the old project — I adopted its
  boxed-tabs-with-superscript-hotkeys style as a mockup variant
  (tabbar-v2-boxed), and noted its 3-layer router→view→widget architecture and
  the huh form library in WORK.md as the basis for the real implementation
  later.

  Everything renders via the static harness (all screens verified ≤ 80 columns):

  go run ./cmd/mockup -list            # list all screens
  go run ./cmd/mockup <name> [WxH]     # render one (default 80x60)

  I'll present three sets and pause after each.

  Set 1 of 3 — tab bar style (both shown over the same folder-table body):

  go run ./cmd/mockup tabbar-v1-underline 80x24
  go run ./cmd/mockup tabbar-v2-boxed 80x24

  - v1-underline: single-line tab row (Folders │ This Device │ …), active tab
  bold+underlined, separator rule below. Costs 2 rows.
  - v2-boxed: your old project's style — active tab in a border box, superscript
  hotkeys ¹²³⁴⁵ so number keys jump straight to a tab. Costs 3 rows but more
  visually distinct.

  Coming after your feedback: Set 2 — Folders tab layouts (folders-v1-panels,
  folders-v2-table, folders-v3-split), then Set 3 — Alerts tab (alerts-v1-list,
  alerts-v2-focus).

  Which tab bar style do you prefer (or what would you change)?

      283 +func devicesSplit(width, height int) string {                 
      284 +  leftW := 26                                                 
      285 +  var list strings.Builder                                    
      286 +  for i, d := range fakeDevices {                             
      287 +    cursor, style := "  ", lipgloss.NewStyle()                
      288 +    if i == 0 {                                               
      289 +      cursor, style = keyStyle.Render("▸ "), lipgloss.NewStyle
          +().Bold(true)                                                 
      290 +    }                                                         
      291 +    dot := stateStyle(d.State).Render("●")                    
      292 +    list.WriteString(cursor + dot + " " + style.Render(d.Name)
          + + "\n")                                                      
      293 +  }                                                           
      294 +  list.WriteString("\n  " + keyStyle.Render("+") + " Add Devic
          +e\n  " + keyStyle.Render("≡") + " Recent Changes\n")          
      295 +  sel := fakeDevices[0]                                       
      296 +  detail := lipgloss.NewStyle().Bold(true).Render(sel.Name) + 
          +"\n\n" +                                                      
      297 +    fmt.Sprintf("State            %s\n", stateStyle(sel.State)
          +.Render(sel.State)) +                                         
      298 +    fmt.Sprintf("Address          %s\n", sel.Address) +       
      299 +    fmt.Sprintf("Download Rate    %s\n", sel.Download) +      
      300 +    fmt.Sprintf("Upload Rate      %s\n", sel.Upload) +        
      301 +    "Compression      Metadata Only\n" +                      
      302 +    "Introducer       No\n" +                                 
      303 +    "Last Seen        2026-07-12 10:41\n" +                   
      304 +    "Version          v2.0.13\n" +                            
      305 +    "Folders          Documents, Photos, Music\n\n" +         
      306 +    progressBar(sel.Pct, width-leftW-8) + fmt.Sprintf(" %d%%\n
          +\n", sel.Pct) +                                               
      307 +    buttonRow([]string{"Pause", "Edit"}, 0)

          +.BorderForeground(dim).                                       
      311 +    Render(list.String())

          +\n" + thisDeviceStats[i][1])                                  
      350 +    r := box.Render(dimStyle.Render(thisDeviceStats[i+1][0]) +
          + "\n" + thisDeviceStats[i+1][1])                              
      351 +    rows = append(rows, lipgloss.JoinHorizontal(lipgloss.Top, 
          +l, r))                                                        
      352 +  }                                                           
      353 +  keys := keyHint("1-5", "switch tab", "q", "quit")           
      354 +  return frame("This Device", strings.Join(rows, "\n"), keys, 
          +width, height)

      397 +// editFolderForm renders the shared form body: sub-tab row, G
          +eneral fields,                                                
      398 +// bottom buttons. First field focused.                       
      399 +func editFolderForm(width int) string {                       
      400 +  subTabs := []string{"General", "Versioning", "Ignore Pattern
          +s", "Advanced"}                                               
      401 +  var tabs []string                                           
      402 +  for i, t := range subTabs {                                 
      403 +    style := dimStyle                                         
      404 +    if i == 0 {                                               
      405 +      style = lipgloss.NewStyle().Bold(true).Foreground(accent
          +).Underline(true)                                             
      406 +    }                                                         
      407 +    tabs = append(tabs, style.Render(t))                      
      408 +  }                                                           
      409 +  field := func(label, value, desc string, focused bool) strin
          +g {                                                           
      410 +    box := lipgloss.NewStyle().Foreground(dim)                
      411 +    if focused {                                              
      412 +      box = lipgloss.NewStyle().Foreground(accent)            
      413 +    }                                                         
      414 +    s := fmt.Sprintf("  %-14s %s\n", label, box.Render("▕ "+fm
          +t.Sprintf("%-40s", value)+"▏"))                               
      415 +    return s + "                 " + dimStyle.Render(desc) + "
          +\n\n"                                                         
      416 +  }                                                           
      417 +  return strings.Join(tabs, dimStyle.Render("  │  ")) + "\n" +
      418 +    dimStyle.Render(strings.Repeat("─", width)) + "\n\n" +    
      419 +    field("Folder Label", "foobar", "Optional label, can diffe
          +r per device", true) +                                        
      420 +    field("Folder ID", "ab7cd-ef8gh (readonly)", "Same on all 
          +cluster devices", false) +                                    
      421 +    field("Folder Path", "~/foobar", "Created if it does not e
          +xist", false) +

          +)

          +.HiddenBorder()).Bold(true)

          +NormalBorder()).BorderForeground(accent)                      
      430 +  title := titleSt.Render("Edit Folder " + dimStyle.Render("(f
          +oobar)"))                                                     
      431 +  back := backSt.Render("Back" + dimStyle.Render("ᵉˢᶜ"))      
      432 +  gap := width - lipgloss.Width(title) - lipgloss.Width(back) 
      433 +  top := lipgloss.JoinHorizontal(lipgloss.Bottom, title, strin
          +gs.Repeat(" ", max(0, gap)), back)                            
      434 +  bottom := statusBar(keyHint("↑↓", "field", "tab", "next sect
          +ion", "enter", "edit", "esc", "back"), "↓1.2MiB/s ↑2.4MiB/s",

      397 +// ── Subview building blocks ────────────────────────────────
          +──────────────────                                            
      398 +                                                              
      399 +// subTabRow renders sub-tabs in the same boxed style as the m
          +ain tab bar,                                                  
      400 +// with superscript number hotkeys.                           
      401 +func subTabRow(names []string, active int) string {           
      402 +  sups := []string{"¹", "²", "³", "⁴", "⁵", "⁶", "⁷", "⁸", "⁹"
          +}                                                             
      403 +  inactive := lipgloss.NewStyle().Padding(0, 1).Border(lipglos
          +s.HiddenBorder())                                             
      404 +  activeSt := lipgloss.NewStyle().Padding(0, 1).Border(lipglos
          +s.NormalBorder()).BorderForeground(accent)                    
      405 +  tabs := make([]string, len(names))                          
      406 +  for i, n := range names {                                   
      407 +    style := inactive                                         
      408 +    if i == active {                                          
      409 +      style = activeSt                                        
      410 +    }                                                         
      411 +    tabs[i] = style.Render(n + dimStyle.Render(sups[i]))      
      412 +  }                                                           
      413 +  return lipgloss.JoinHorizontal(lipgloss.Bottom, tabs...)    
      414 +}                                                             
      415 +                                                              
      416 +// formField renders a labeled input with dim description belo
          +w.                                                            
      417 +// NOTE: placeholder styling — user wants the ▕value▏ look rev
          +isited later.                                                 
      418 +func formField(label, value, desc string, focused bool) string
          + {                                                            
      419 +  box := dimStyle                                             
      420 +  if focused {                                                
      421 +    box = lipgloss.NewStyle().Foreground(accent)              
      422 +  }                                                           
      423 +  return fmt.Sprintf("  %-14s %s\n", label, box.Render("▕ "+fm
          +t.Sprintf("%-40s", value)+"▏")) +                             
      424 +    "                 " + dimStyle.Render(desc) + "\n\n"      
      425 +}                                                             
      426 +                                                              
      427 +// checkField renders a checkbox field with dim description be
          +low.                                                          
      428 +func checkField(label string, checked bool, desc string, focus
          +ed bool) string {                                             
      429 +  mark := "[ ]"                                               
      430 +  if checked {                                                
      431 +    mark = "[x]"                                              
      432 +  }                                                           
      433 +  style := lipgloss.NewStyle()                                
      434 +  if focused {                                                
      435 +    style = lipgloss.NewStyle().Foreground(accent)            
      436 +  }                                                           
      437 +  return fmt.Sprintf("  %-14s %s\n", label, style.Render(mark)
          +) +                                                           
      438 +    "                 " + dimStyle.Render(desc) + "\n\n"      
      439 +}                                                             
      440 +                                                              
      441 +// infobarFrame is the chosen subview chrome: same-height info
          + bar with the                                                 
      442 +// title on the left and a boxed Back button (esc) on the righ
          +t.                                                            
      443 +func infobarFrame(title, sub, body, keys string, width, height
          + int) string {                                                
      444 +  titleSt := lipgloss.NewStyle().Padding(0, 1).Border(lipgloss
          +.HiddenBorder()).Bold(true)                                   
      445 +  backSt := lipgloss.NewStyle().Padding(0, 1).Border(lipgloss.
          +NormalBorder()).BorderForeground(accent)                      
      446 +  left := titleSt.Render(title + " " + dimStyle.Render("("+sub
          ++")"))                                                        
      447 +  back := backSt.Render("Back" + dimStyle.Render("ᵉˢᶜ"))      
      448 +  gap := width - lipgloss.Width(left) - lipgloss.Width(back)  
      449 +  top := lipgloss.JoinHorizontal(lipgloss.Bottom, left, string
          +s.Repeat(" ", max(0, gap)), back)                             
      450 +  bottom := statusBar(keys, "↓1.2MiB/s ↑2.4MiB/s", width)     
      451 +  used := lipgloss.Height(top) + lipgloss.Height(bottom)      
      452 +  body = lipgloss.NewStyle().Width(width).Height(height - used
          +).Render(body)                                                
      453 +  return lipgloss.JoinVertical(lipgloss.Left, top, body, botto
          +m)                                                            
      454 +}                                                             
      455 +                                                              
      456 +var subviewKeys = keyHint("↑↓", "field", "tab", "next section"
          +, "enter", "edit", "esc", "back")                             
      457 +

      465 +  return infobarFrame("Edit Device", "workpc", body, subviewKe
          +ys, width, height)                                            
      466 +}

● Done. formField now takes a readonly flag: readonly fields (Folder ID, Device
  ID, API Key) render fully greyed out with no ▕ ▏ input affordance, while
  editable fields keep the input marker — same visual contract as the web GUI's
  greyed-out fields. The constraint is also noted in WORK.md so the future
  field-style redesign preserves the distinction. All three subview screens
  rebuilt cleanly:

  go run ./cmd/mockup editfolder-v1-infobar
  go run ./cmd/mockup editdevice-infobar
  go run ./cmd/mockup settings-infobar

  remaining sub-tab contents (Versioning, Ignore Patterns, Advanced, GUI,
  Connections) to complete the full walkthrough mockup.

● Update(mockups/screens.go)
  ⎿  Added 34 lines
      586    return infobarFrame("Edit Folder", "foobar", body, subviewKe
           ys, width, height)
      587  }
      588  
      589 +// fakeQR renders a placeholder QR (finder patterns + noise) w
          +ith half-block                                                
      590 +// characters, 2 modules per row. The real TUI will render an 
          +actual QR the                                                 
      591 +// same way (e.g. skip2/go-qrcode).                           
      592 +func fakeQR(n int) string {                                   
      593 +  mod := func(x, y int) bool {                                
      594 +    finder := func(cx, cy int) bool {                         
      595 +      dx, dy := x-cx, y-cy                                    
      596 +      if dx < 0 || dy < 0 || dx > 6 || dy > 6 {               
      597 +        return false                                          
      598 +      }                                                       
      599 +      edge := dx == 0 || dy == 0 || dx == 6 || dy == 6        
      600 +      core := dx >= 2 && dx <= 4 && dy >= 2 && dy <= 4        
      601 +      return edge || core                                     
      602 +    }                                                         
      603 +    if finder(0, 0) || finder(n-7, 0) || finder(0, n-7) {     
      604 +      return true                                             
      605 +    }                                                         
      606 +    return (x*7+y*13+x*y)%5 < 2 // deterministic noise        
      607 +  }                                                           
      608 +  var b strings.Builder                                       
      609 +  for y := 0; y < n; y += 2 {                                 
      610 +    for x := 0; x < n; x++ {                                  
      611 +      top, bot := mod(x, y), y+1 < n && mod(x, y+1)           
      612 +      b.WriteString(map[[2]bool]string{                       
      613 +        {true, true}: "█", {true, false}: "▀",                
      614 +        {false, true}: "▄", {false, false}: " ",              
      615 +      }[[2]bool{top, bot}])                                   
      616 +    }                                                         
      617 +    b.WriteString("\n")                                       
      618 +  }                                                           
      619 +  return strings.TrimRight(b.String(), "\n")                  
      620 +}                                                             
      621 +                                                              
      622  func showID(width, height int) string {
      623    id := "MFZWI3D-BONSGYC-YLTMRWG-C43ENR5-QXGZDMM-FZWI3DP-BONSG
           YC-YLTMRW4"
      624    idBox := lipgloss.NewStyle().Border(lipgloss.RoundedBorder()
           ).BorderForeground(accent).
     ...
      626    body := lipgloss.Place(width, height-8, lipgloss.Center, lip
           gloss.Center,
      627      lipgloss.JoinVertical(lipgloss.Center,
      628        "Share this ID with other devices to connect.", "", idBo
           x, "",
      629 +      fakeQR(25), "",                                         
      630        buttonRow([]string{"Copy", "Close"}, 0)))
      631    return infobarFrame("Show ID", "this-machine", body,
      632      keyHint("←→", "select", "enter", "confirm", "esc", "back")
           , width, height)

● Stage 3 is built — the full interactive mockup is ready to walk through:

  go run ./cmd/syncthingtui              # interactive (q to quit)
  go run ./cmd/syncthingtui -list       # static screen names
  go run ./cmd/syncthingtui -screen edit-folder -size 80x34

  What works interactively:
  - All 5 tabs via number keys 1–5; ↑↓/jk selection in Folders, Remote Devices,
  Alerts, Actions; detail panes update live from fake data.
  - enter on a folder/device opens its Edit subview; on + Add Folder/+ Add 
  Device opens the Add variant; esc returns.
  - Alerts: ←→ picks Add/Ignore/Dismiss, enter resolves the alert (removes it,
  tab badge count updates; "Add Device" chains into the Add Device form).
  - Actions → Settings / Show ID open their subviews; Show ID has the QR.

● Both changes are in:

  1. tab/⇧tab now cycles the main tabs (wrapping), the 1-5:tab hints are gone
  everywhere, and the hint reads tab/⇧tab:change tabs. Number keys still work,
  matching the superscripts. Side effect handled: the longer hints didn't fit
  alongside the net-rates on busy tabs, so the status bar now drops the rates
  when space is tight — hints win.
  2. Remote Devices (and Folders, for consistency) detail panes now end in real
  button rows — devices: [ Pause ] [ Edit ], folders: [ Pause ] [ Rescan ] [ 
  Edit ] — navigated with ←→, activated with enter. Pause actually toggles the
  fake state (button flips to Resume, list dot goes grey).
  3. On huh and browse-then-edit navigation: huh is too opinionated for that.
  Its model is "one field is always live" — there's no browse mode, so j/k would
  literally type j and k into text inputs, and enter means "advance to next
  field", not "start editing". You can rebind keys via WithKeyMap, but you can't
  add the modal browse/edit separation without fighting it. The clean path is a
  small custom form layer built on bubbles primitives (textinput, textarea,
  plus trivial select/checkbox widgets): ↑↓/jk moves a highlight, enter focuses
  the field for editing, esc/enter commits back to browse mode. That also gives
  us full control of field styling — which you already wanted revisited anyway.
  It's maybe 150–250 lines, written once and shared by all three form subviews.

  Want me to replace the huh forms with that custom browse/edit form layer?

✻ Cooked for 3m 20s

※ recap: Building a syncthing TUI in Go/bubbletea; the interactive stage-3 mockup with all tabs and forms is working. Awaiting your decision on replacing huh forms with a custom browse/edit form layer (jk navigate, enter to edit).

❯ yes, I think browse-then-edit with huh is necessary for this GUI.  a question 
  though, huh has multi-select (e.g. Remote Devices tab in syncthingtui_old).   
  Can we not stuff our form fields into those, or not clean?                    
                                                                                
  side note:  I notice if I hit enter until I get to the end of a huh-form the  
  whole form just disappears                                                    

● Good instinct on both counts — let me answer, then build it.

  Multi-select as field container: not clean. huh.MultiSelect/Select give you
  exactly the browse feel you want (j/k over rows, enter acts), but their
  options are static strings with a checkmark toggle — there's no way to embed
  an editable value, live-render "Label → current value" per row, or launch an
  editor on enter without swapping components mid-flight. You'd be using it as a
  menu and reimplementing all the field logic anyway, at which point the menu
  is the easy part to write ourselves.

  Disappearing form: that's huh's wizard model — after the last field, the form
  transitions to StateCompleted and View() renders nothing. Your old project hit
  this too (form_widget.go recreates the form on completion as a workaround). A
  persistent settings panel is just not huh's shape; a third strike alongside
  j/k and enter semantics.

  So: replacing huh with a custom browse-then-edit form layer on bubbles
  primitives.

● huh is out, replaced by a custom browse-then-edit form layer (app/form.go,
  ~250 lines) shared by all three form subviews:

  Interaction model — exactly what you asked for:
  - Browse mode: ↑↓/jk moves the ▸ cursor across fields (and onto the button
  row), the focused field shows its description; tab/⇧tab still switches
  sections; esc leaves the subview.
  - enter on a field starts editing: text/password fields get a live cursor
  (bubbles textinput), selects expand into an inline option list navigated with
  jk, checkboxes just toggle without an edit mode, the ignore-patterns textarea
  opens multi-line (enter = newline, so ctrl+s commits there).
  - While editing: enter commits, esc discards — and esc while editing cancels
  the edit only, not the subview. The status-bar hints switch contextually to
  show this.
  - Buttons: cursor down to [ Save ] [ Close ] [ Remove ], ←→ + enter — they
  close with a flash message for now (nothing persists until stage 4).

  Your two observations confirmed the switch: the multi-select trick isn't clean
  — huh options are static strings with a checkmark, so you can't render "Label
  → value" rows or hook enter to per-field editors without rebuilding all the
  field logic around it. And the vanishing form was huh's wizard design
  (StateCompleted renders empty; your old project's form_widget.go worked around
  it by recreating the form). Both problems are structural, both gone now — the
  custom Form never "completes."

  Also fixed while in there: long labels ("Minimum Free Disk Space") were
  colliding with values; the label column is now 32 chars. All screens still
  pass the 80-col static check (checker now strips ANSI codes to avoid false
  positives).

  go run ./cmd/syncthingtui to try it — Settings or Edit Folder are the best
  tours of all the field kinds.

✻ Baked for 2m 56s

❯ - put "Edit" as the first option any time it is present as a button selected  
  with left/right arrows.  also make "esc" also work to quit on mainview (you   
  can leave hint alone).                                                        
  - in Edit Folder view (and maybe others), arrow keys dont work to select tabs 
  anymore                                                                       
  - why is Edit Folder missing the Sharing tab?  is it missing from GUI.yaml?   

● All three — the Sharing answer first: it's not missing from GUI.yaml, it's
  listed there with a # do not implement marker (line 73, "Device sharing with
  encryption passwords…"), which the original mockups honored. Since you
  expected it, I'll implement the basic part (share-with-device checkboxes) and
  keep only the encryption passwords out of scope, updating GUI.yaml to match.

  wiring):

  1. API client — small package for syncthing's REST interface (X-API-Key auth):
  config, status, connections, db-status endpoints, plus /rest/events
  long-polling so the UI updates live.
  2. Live data — swap app/data.go fakes for real folders/devices/stats; forms
  load from and POST back to /rest/config.
  3. Actions — wire pause/resume/rescan, alert accept/ignore/dismiss
  (/rest/cluster/pending/*), Save/Remove on forms, restart/shutdown.
  4. Integration tests — spin up a throwaway local syncthing instance (temp home
  dir) and assert each TUI action produces the right REST effect, per
  AGENTS.md.
  5. Upstream parity tooling — a check that compares the TUI's form fields
  against syncthing's config structs so future syncthing releases flag drift;
  worth a short design discussion when we get there.

  Suggested order is 1→2 first (read-only TUI against a real instance is a good
  milestone), then 3, with 4 growing alongside. Say the word and I'll start on
  the client.

● Stage 4 first milestone is done and verified end-to-end:

  Built:
  - client/ — minimal syncthing REST client (config, status, connections,
  db-status, version, pending devices/folders, device stats/completion, rescan,
  pause/resume via PATCH, dismiss-pending via DELETE), plus Discover() which
  reads the GUI address and API key from your local config.xml (both new and
  legacy locations).
  - Live mode in the app — App polls every 3s and feeds real data into the
  existing views: folders (state/sync% derived from db-status + config), remote
  devices (connection state, completion %, compression, last-seen), This Device
  stats (rates computed from byte-total deltas, listeners/discovery counts,
  uptime, ID, version), and Alerts built from the pending endpoints. The
  status-bar rates are live too.
  - Wired actions: pause/resume folder and device, rescan one/all, alert Dismiss
  — each hits the REST API then re-fetches; fake mode still works for static
  rendering and the -screen presets.
  - CLI: go run ./cmd/syncthingtui auto-discovers your local syncthing;
  -address/-api-key override; -fake for the mockup data.

  Verified with ./scripts/test_live.sh: spins up a throwaway syncthing (temp
  home in /tmp, random port, forced test API key — your real instance
  untouched), runs scripts/livecheck against it — all endpoints pass including
  the pause→config-reflects→resume round-trip, on your v1.18 syncthing.

  You can now run go run ./cmd/syncthingtui against your real syncthing for a
  read-mostly look (pause/rescan/dismiss are live — Save on forms is not wired
  yet, so edits are harmless).

  Remaining (logged in WORK.md): form Save/Remove → config PATCH, accepting
  pending devices/folders (Add/Share/Ignore), events long-poll to replace
  polling, real Show ID/restart/shutdown, porting the smoke test to proper go 
  test, and the upstream-parity tooling discussion.

● Fixed — the problem was TLS: your GUI has tls="true" in config.xml, syncthing
  307-redirects http:// API calls to https://, and its self-signed cert (expired
  June 2026, incidentally) failed Go's verification, so every fetch errored out
  and the lists stayed empty.

  Changes:
  - Discover() now reads the tls attribute and returns an https:// base URL when
  set.
  - The client accepts full URLs and skips certificate verification — standard
  practice for syncthing's self-signed GUI cert; the API key remains the actual
  authentication.
  - Live mode now shows "connecting to syncthing…" on This Device instead of the
  fake stats before the first poll completes.

  Verified: the read-only check against your real instance now returns your 3
  folders (default, tikgame, research) and 5 devices (tikhonov, blackbox,
  SM-P610, cubebox + self, which is filtered out of the Remote Devices list).
  The throwaway-instance smoke test and all static width checks still pass.

  go run ./cmd/syncthingtui should now populate Folders and Remote Devices. Also
  worth noting: any fetch error now shows in the status bar's right corner
  (that's where the earlier failure was silently hiding — it flashed the TLS
  error but the text was easy to miss).

● All three done:

  Notice/OK semantics — the REST API has no per-error clear (only
  /rest/system/error/clear, all-at-once), so per your fallback: all system
  errors now combine into one Notice alert, each line timestamped, separated by
  blank lines. OK clears the whole list, which now matches what you see.
  GUI.yaml documents the constraint.

  Tests — honest answer: until this turn, coverage was the scripts/test_live.sh
  integration smoke (throwaway syncthing, all GET endpoints + pause/resume
  round-trip) plus the static 80-column render checks; no Go unit tests yet
  (porting the smoke into proper go test is stage-4 item 5). I did add the
  notice flow to the smoke test just now: post error → visible via GET → clear →
  empty, all passing. The pause/rescan/dismiss UI paths aren't covered — only
  the client calls beneath them.

  About view — added About to the Actions menu (after Support Bundle, matching
  web GUI order, GUI.yaml updated):

✻ Baked for 2m 43s

❯ whats the ultimate plan for tests?  how extensive should our tests be?        
                                                                                
  I hope to keep this code maintained, either through agents or knowledgable go 
  programmers.  do we need to build up a lot of docs for agents/programmers to  
  read, or are things self-evident?                                             
                                                                                
  be concise                                                                    
● Test plan — three layers, matched to what actually breaks:

  1. Static layout tests (cheap, high value): port the width-check loop into go 
  test — render every screen preset at 80×60 and a couple of other sizes, assert
  ≤80 cols; optionally golden-file snapshots so layout regressions show as
  diffs. This is your AGENTS.md "agent can review any screen" requirement made
  permanent.
  2. Integration tests (the core): port livecheck into go test with a TestMain
  harness that spawns the throwaway syncthing. Grow it per wired action — every
  REST-touching feature gets an assert (pause → config reflects it, OK → errors
  empty, Save → config changed). This is the layer that catches real bugs, and
  syncthing-version drift.
  3. Unit tests (sparingly): only logic with branches worth pinning —
  folderState() mapping, humanBytes, form browse/edit key handling. No tests for
  plumbing like viewFolders string assembly; the render tests cover those.

  Skip: mocked-HTTP client tests (the real instance is cheap), exhaustive UI
  key-event simulation. Rule of thumb going forward: every wired action = one
  integration assert; every screen = one render check; that's ~80% of the value
  for ~20% of the effort.

  Docs — mostly self-evident, don't build a doc mountain. The code is small
  (~1500 lines), idiomatic bubbletea, and WORK.md already carries the decision
  history. What's worth having, and it's thin:

  - CLAUDE.md (~1 page, doubles for humans): architecture in five lines (App →
  MainModel/subviews → Form; live.go polls → dataMsg), the commands
  (-screen/-list, test_live.sh), the invariants (80-col rule, keep rejected
  mockups, readonly-field distinction, WORK.md protocol), and "GUI.yaml is the
  parity spec — update it when scope changes."
  - The upstream-parity tooling (stage-4 item 6) is the real maintainability
  lever — a check that diffs TUI form fields against syncthing's config schema
  does more for future maintainers than any prose.

  Everything else — endpoint choices, workarounds like the v1.18 quirks —
  belongs as short comments at the point of use, which is largely already the
  case. Want me to write that CLAUDE.md and port the tests to go test as the
  next chunk?

✻ Baked for 17s

❯ explain what you mean by upstream-parity tooling.  what is "syncthing's       
  config schema"?  is this related to GUI structure?  be concise                

● Syncthing's config schema = the data model behind /rest/config — upstream Go
  structs in ../syncthing/lib/config (FolderConfiguration, DeviceConfiguration,
  OptionsConfiguration, GUIConfiguration). Every field in the web GUI's Edit
  Folder/Edit Device/Settings forms reads and writes one key in that JSON (e.g.
  our "Full Rescan Interval" ↔ rescanIntervalS).

  It's related to but distinct from GUI structure: GUI.yaml describes visual
  layout (tabs, buttons, alerts); the schema is the data those forms edit. Forms
  are ~70% of the parity surface, and they're the machine-checkable part.

  The tooling: give each of our form fields an explicit mapping to its config
  key, then a small tool that:
  1. fetches /rest/config from a given syncthing version (or parses the structs
  from ../syncthing),
  2. diffs both directions:
    - schema keys we don't expose → new upstream features, candidates to add;
    - keys we map that no longer exist → renamed/removed upstream, our form is
  now broken.

  Run it in CI or manually after git pull in ../syncthing; output is a short
  "drift report". That's the AGENTS.md "straightforward updating to follow
  syncthing changes" requirement made concrete: an agent bumping to syncthing
  v2.1 runs the tool and gets a to-do list instead of eyeballing release notes.

  What it can't catch: non-config surfaces — new alert types, new REST actions,
  new modals. Those stay manual: GUI.yaml is the checklist, and a diff of
  upstream's gui/default/ between versions is the discovery mechanism.

✻ Brewed for 17s

❯ does the tool provide significantly more value than an instruction in         
  AGENTS.md of where to check in syncthing upstream for changes?  dont want to  
  add a lot of code for something which might be brittle                        

● Honestly — no, probably not. The tool's real value decomposes into two parts,
  and the cheap part carries most of it:

  1. The mapping (field → config key): valuable, but you get it nearly free by
  naming the config key in the form-field definitions or a comment — no tool
  needed, and it makes any manual/agent audit mechanical.
  2. The automated diff: this is the brittle part. It needs to parse upstream
  structs or normalize live JSON, handle renames vs removals, filter dozens of
  deliberately-unexposed keys (an ignore-list that itself rots), and it still
  misses the non-config surface (alerts, modals, actions). That's real code with
  real maintenance for a check that runs a few times a year.

  An agent with a good instruction does the same job nearly as reliably: fetch
  /rest/config, compare keys against our mapped fields, diff gui/default/
  between versions for the rest. That's exactly the kind of task agents are good
  at when told where to look.

  So my recommendation: skip the tool. Instead:
  - annotate each form field with its config key (one-line change per field),
  - record in WORK.md which syncthing version we're synced against,
  - add a short "syncing with upstream" section to AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md: "Compare
  /rest/config keys against the mapped fields in app/subviews.go; diff 
  ../syncthing/gui/default/ since the version noted in WORK.md; GUI.yaml is the 
  parity checklist — update it with any scope change."

  If parity audits later turn out to be frequent and tedious, the mapping
  annotations mean the tool can be written then with no rework. Want me to do
  those three cheap pieces?

✻ Sautéed for 14s

  dont refer to WORK.md                                                         
                                                                                
  proceed

      57 +  return f

      126  // buttonMsg reports an activated form button ("Save", "Close"

      129 +// values collects the savable fields into config-key → value.
      130 +func (f Form) values() map[string]any {                       
      131 +  m := map[string]any{}                                       
      132 +  for _, fld := range f.fields {                              
      133 +    if fld.key == "" || fld.kind == ftRO {                    
      134 +      continue                                                
      135 +    }                                                         
      136 +    switch fld.kind {                                         
      137 +    case ftBool:                                              
      138 +      m[fld.key] = fld.boolVal                                
      139 +    case ftSelect:                                            
      140 +      if len(fld.vals) == len(fld.options) {                  
      141 +        m[fld.key] = fld.vals[fld.optIdx]                     
      142 +      } else {                                                
      143 +        m[fld.key] = fld.options[fld.optIdx]                  
      144 +      }                                                       
      145 +    default: // text, pass, area                              
      146 +      if fld.numeric {                                        
      147 +        n, _ := strconv.Atoi(strings.TrimSpace(fld.value))    
      148 +        m[fld.key] = n                                        
      149 +      } else {                                                
      150 +        m[fld.key] = fld.value                                
      151 +      }                                                       
      152 +    }                                                         
      153 +  }                                                           
      154 +  return m                                                    
      155 +}                                                             
      156 +

      159      f.width = ws.Width

          +ool) tea.Cmd {

       79 +  }

       84 +      return dataMsg{err: err}                                
       85 +    }

● Form editing is now fully live for folders and devices. Summary:

  What changed:
  - Editors load real config on open: Edit Folder fetches the folder's config +
  ignore patterns + device list; Edit Device fetches the device config. No more
  hardcoded form values in live mode.
  - Config keys are now functional: fields declare .K("rescanIntervalS"),
  selects map display↔config values via .Vals("sendreceive", …), numeric fields
  marked .Num() — these drive Save and serve as the upstream-parity mapping
  (AGENTS.md procedure updated accordingly).
  - Save works: existing folder/device → PATCH 
  /rest/config/{folders,devices}/{id} with the changed schema keys (versioning
  assembled as a nested object, sharing checkboxes → devices[], ignore patterns
  → POST /rest/db/ignores); new folder/device → POST (new folders get a
  web-GUI-style generated ID, editable). Remove → DELETE. Closing after save
  triggers an immediate refetch so the main view reflects it.
  - Alert "Add Device" now genuinely accepts a pending device — opens Add Device
  prefilled with the pending ID; Save POSTs it to the config.
  - Settings Save is still unwired (flashes "not wired"): its mappings are
  fiddlier (minHomeDiskFree is an object, urAccepted is magic ints, upgrades
  split across two keys) — top of the remaining list along with Share
  Folder/Ignore alert actions.

  Verified: integration test extended with full folder lifecycle (create → patch
  → verify → ignores round-trip → delete) and device lifecycle (add → patch →
  verify → delete, using a second generated syncthing identity for a valid
  check-digit ID — my fabricated test ID got a 400, which was itself a good
  validation check). All green, plus width checks.

  Careful note for live use: Save/Remove now really modify your syncthing config
  — Remove on a real folder is destructive (though it doesn't delete files on
  disk).

✻ Churned for 6m 18s
